---
title: "2026-08-12 - Anti-abortion rights group says Idaho hasn’t lost OB-GYNs. Doctors push back - idahostatesman.com"
source: "Idaho Statesman"
author: "[[Laura Guido]]"
published: 2026-08-12

![Blaine Conzatti, president of the Christian advocacy group Idaho Family Policy Center, speaks at an anti-abortion rights rally at the Idaho Capitol in 2026 — four years after Idaho’s strict abortion ban went into effect making it illegal in the state.](https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_1200/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg)

Blaine Conzatti, president of the Christian advocacy group Idaho Family Policy Center, speaks at an anti-abortion rights rally at the Idaho Capitol in 2026 — four years after Idaho’s strict abortion ban went into effect making it illegal in the state. smiller@idahostatesman.com

Listen to this article

Dr. Kylie Cooper, a maternal-fetal medicine physician, practices in Minnesota after she left Idaho in the spring of 2023, shortly after Idaho’s criminal abortion ban went into effect.

Although it has been nearly three years since she left, Cooper retained her Idaho medical license, she said.

“I have a sliver of hope that maybe things will get better in the future,” Cooper said.

Cooper’s license, as well as every other active medical OB-GYN license in the state, would have shown up in data recently shared by the Idaho Family Policy Center — a Christian advocacy group that lobbied for Idaho’s criminal abortion ban and helped author a 2022 law allowing family members of a fetus aborted after six weeks to [sue the abortion provider](https://idahocapitalsun.com/2022/03/14/texas-style-abortion-bill-heads-to-idaho-gov-brad-littles-desk/) for a minimum of $20,000.

As campaigns for and against [Proposition 1](https://archive.sos.idaho.gov/elections/initiatives/2026/RFPA.pdf) — a citizen-led ballot initiative that would repeal the state’s strict abortion ban and provide protections for contraception and in vitro fertilization — ramp up for the Nov. 3 general election, the abortion law’s effect on the number of practicing OB-GYNs in Idaho has become a talking point. But determining the true number of doctors providing obstetrician-gynecologist care in the state isn’t easy.

The state has said the number of active licenses do not reliably reflect how many OB-GYNs are currently practicing in Idaho.

The Idaho Family Policy Center has underscored the licensing data to question, and file a [lawsuit](https://idahofamily.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/2026-07-24-Petition-and-Complaint.pdf) over, a 2025 Boise State University [study](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamanetworkopen/fullarticle/2837058) that found Idaho had lost 35% of its OB-GYNs since 2022, when the U.S. Supreme Court overturned Roe v. Wade and Idaho’s criminal abortion law went into effect.

The policy center on Aug. 3 [posted](https://www.facebook.com/share/p/1BbKZPA2iF/) a graph on Facebook showing the number of active licenses increasing from 2022 to 2025, writing, “Pro-abortion advocates are lying to you.”

“The number of actively licensed OB/GYNs in Idaho has actually increased since our pro-life laws went into effect, according to data from the Idaho Board of Medicine,” the post said.

The center’s posts about the licensure numbers have been shared by Idaho state legislators, such as Twin Falls Republican Rep. Clint Hostetler and Middleton Republican Rep. Tammy Nichols.

## Idaho medical board says it has no ‘reliable way’ to count active physicians

The Idaho Board of Medicine, which is under the state Division of Occupational and Professional Licenses, shows the number of active OB-GYN licenses in Idaho going from around 280 in 2022 to just under 340 in 2024, according to the policy center’s [graph](https://idahofamily.org/pro-abortion-activists-are-lying-obgyns-arent-leaving-idaho-after-all/).

Bob McLaughlin, spokesperson at the Division of Occupational and Professional Licenses, known as DOPL, told the Idaho Capital Sun that there are limitations to the state’s data to try and determine how many doctors are practicing in the state.

“One, it’s a two-year license,” McLaughlin said in an email. “The number of licenses could fluctuate since the licensee is required to renew on their birthdate every two years and not annually. Two, physician specialty information is self-reported and is not required as part of licensure. DOPL does not have a reliable way to determine how many licensed physicians are actively practicing in Idaho. Three, our records reflect licensure status, and an active license does not necessarily mean an individual is currently practicing.”

Family policy center President Blaine Conzatti [wrote on the organization’s website](https://idahofamily.org/pro-abortion-activists-are-lying-obgyns-arent-leaving-idaho-after-all/), noting that the data from the Board of Medicine is “admittedly imperfect,” citing many of the same limitations McLaughlin named, including that not all physicians practice in states where their license is active and that specialties are self-reported.

“Even still, it’s the best data available,” Conzatti wrote. “And it clearly shows that the number of Idaho OB/GYNs hasn’t been decreasing at the rate that JAMA article researchers — and their boosters in the media — have been telling you.”

The 2025 study, published in the Journal of the American Medical Association, found that Idaho experienced a net loss of 94 out of 264 OB-GYN physicians between August 2022 and December 2024.

Those numbers were compiled from “from publicly available credentialing files and sources, licensing boards, physician websites, Idaho’s American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists registry, Idaho Medical Association database, local knowledge of the practices, and direct physician confirmation,” the study’s abstract said.

The researchers tracked relocations, those who stopped practicing obstetrics, died or retired, and counted newly credentialed doctors, the study said. It did not include temporary physicians or family medicine doctors who also practice obstetrics.

“The unique thing about that JAMA Open study was that it was an actual count of actual individuals in Idaho,” said Susie Keller, CEO of the Idaho Medical Association. “It wasn’t an extrapolation. It wasn’t based on estimates. So it was very unique and went through many, many layers of scrutiny before being published.”

Keller said physicians left in that time period for a variety of reasons, some of which included Idaho’s abortion laws but others were personal, such as to be closer to family.

“There are a lot of factors that play into our physician shortage,” Keller said. “And for this particular subset, I think the study did a good job of explaining those different reasons.”

A spokesperson from St. Luke’s, which has hospital clinic locations across southwest Idaho and Twin Falls, told the Idaho Capital Sun there were limitations to using licensing data to try and quantify how many doctors there are practicing.

“While we cannot speak for statewide physician licensing trends, our experience is that licensing figures alone do not necessarily reflect the number of physicians actively providing obstetrical care to Idaho patients or available for long-term practice in Idaho,” spokesperson Joy Prudek said in an email.

She said that licenses may include those who “practice intermittently,” or temporary physicians who fill in gaps, or those who retain licenses from multiple states.

Conzatti told the Sun in an email, that while he knows there are limitations to the licensing data, he questions the JAMA study data.

“We’ve acknowledged from the beginning that the data on actively licensed physicians doesn’t tell the whole story,” he said. “… That being said, we’re willing to bet that the state board data is reliable for our purposes here than the JAMA report for a handful of reasons.”

He said one of reasons is the data from the study provided to the policy center from the records request shows two counties with negative total OB-GYNs.

“That’s impossible — and errors in a data set skew the results,” Conzatti said of the negative doctor counts. “These errors also lead us to question the reliability of the other data relied upon in the report. Unfortunately, because BSU has not complied with our public record request to release the underlying data, communications, and review documents, we can’t thoroughly vet the JAMA report to determine if other data errors exist.”

He also questioned the methodology used by the researchers to collect the data.

“From what we can gather, the authors of the report determined whether a doctor had left based in part on internet searches and asking friends, neither of which are known as robustly scientific processes,” Conzatti said. “Unfortunately, because the authors have still not released their full report and have refused to release the documents we requested, we don’t have further insight into the usefulness of the method and the findings of the study.”

## What are Idaho doctors saying?

Cooper, the maternal-fetal medicine specialist, said she left Idaho because of the threat of prison time under Idaho’s criminal abortion ban and her fear she wouldn’t be able to provide the care she normally would.

As a doctor focused on high-risk pregnancies, she said there were too many potential complications that could warrant an abortion that wouldn’t fall into the law’s exception to prevent the death of the mother or in cases of rape and incest within the first trimester, if there’s a police report filed.

“It was a very difficult decision that we made,” Cooper said. “Thinking through a lot of scenarios, thinking through if I went to prison, what would happen to my husband and children? I mean, it’s just very insane conversations that I never anticipated ever having to have.”

Idaho has had a shortage of doctors for years, said Keller of the Idaho Medical Association. The association has been part of an effort to try and increase physician training opportunities for more than 10 years.

Keller said OB-GYNs have been the focus of tracking because the abortion ban very directly impacted that specialty.

“This particular law that all of a sudden, made evidence-based practice that many of those OB-GYNs had been providing for their patients for many, many years … one day in 2022, it became illegal,” Keller said. “And it very much changed their ability to practice in Idaho.”

Dr. Brenna McCrummen is practicing as an OB-GYN in Coeur d‘Alene, and told the Sun on Monday she feels the pressure from the loss of other specialists in North Idaho. In 2023, Sandpoint’s Bonner General Health [closed its labor and delivery unit](https://idahocapitalsun.com/2023/03/17/citing-staffing-issues-and-political-climate-north-idaho-hospital-will-no-longer-deliver-babies/), citing the political climate and staffing issues.

Many women in the area now must travel for hours to seek care in Coeur d’Alene, McCrummen said. She said this can be especially problematic if a pregnant mom experiences unexpected complications.

“Those problems can get much, much worse the farther out you are,” she said. “Those outcomes for baby and for mom are much worse, and I have definitely seen people arrive to our hospital much sicker than they should be, and that’s all due to the time that they have to travel.”

Prudek, of St. Luke’s, said the health system has made progress in attracting obstetricians and gynecologists, but challenges remain.

“From St. Luke’s perspective, recruiting and retaining OB-GYN physicians remains a priority,” Prudek said. “We have had some success recruiting OB-GYN physicians, particularly by attracting physicians with ties to Idaho and graduates of regional medical education programs. At the same time, our need for OB-GYN physicians continues to exceed the available workforce, and we have experienced limited success recruiting OB hospitalists.”

## Why is the family policy center suing BSU over the study?

The policy center [filed its lawsuit](https://www.idahoednews.org/news/lobbying-group-sues-boise-state-alleging-it-withheld-public-records/) against Boise State University in Ada County District Court on July 24, Idaho EdNews reported.

The advocacy group had sought records related to the 2025 study and its data. Its request returned only a two-page document with the raw data, showing a decline in OB-GYNs.

Conzatti said in the family policy center post announcing the legal challenge that the biggest question for them remained unanswered, “Are OB/GYNs actually leaving the state, as the JAMA study claims? Or is the number of OB/GYNs practicing in Idaho actually increasing, as the data from the Idaho Board of Medicine seems to indicate?”

“This lawsuit means that we’ll finally get the answers,” Conzatti wrote. “And with the upcoming abortion ballot initiative, the need for accurate information has never been more crucial.”

The complaint alleges the university is withholding records, that the response came well after the 10-day deadline required by Idaho public records laws, and that it should not have charged the policy center $257.02 for legal review of the two-page document it received.

Stephany Galbreaith, director of media relations at Boise State, told Idaho EdNews, “We do not comment on pending litigation.”

  <h1 class="h1" tabindex="0">
    
      
      Anti-abortion rights group says Idaho hasn’t lost OB-GYNs. Doctors push back
    
  </h1>

   
  

  
  
    
  





  
  
    
    






    





    









<div class="author-byline">
    <div>
    
    
        
            
                
                
                
                
                   
                    
                                      
                    
                    
                     
                     
                    
                    
                        
                        
                        
                    
                    
                    
    
    
        
            By <span class="author-name">Laura Guido</span>   
            
                <span class="credit">Idaho Capital Sun</span> 
            
            
        
    

                
            
    </div>

            <time datetime="2026-08-12T15:51:19-06:00">August 12, 2026 3:51 PM</time>

</div></header><!----><figure><div class="lead-item ">
        
                







        <div class="img-container picture ">
            
            <img class="responsive-image" src="https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_1200/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg" sizes="(min-width: 992px) 750px, (min-width: 768px) 690px, 100vw" width="1200" height="674" title="Blaine Conzatti, president of the Christian advocacy group Idaho Family Policy Center, speaks at an anti-abortion rights rally at the Idaho Capitol in 2026 — four years after Idaho’s strict abortion ban went into effect making it illegal in the state." alt="Blaine Conzatti, president of the Christian advocacy group Idaho Family Policy Center, speaks at an anti-abortion rights rally at the Idaho Capitol in 2026 — four years after Idaho’s strict abortion ban went into effect making it illegal in the state." srcset="https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_480/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 480w, https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_640/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 640w, https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_768/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 768w, https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_960/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 960w, https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_1140/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 1140w, https://www.idahostatesman.com/public/latest-news/rcmffc/picture314442138/alternates/LANDSCAPE_1200/0124%2005%20March%20for%20Life.jpg 1200w">

                        <figcaption>
                            
    
        Blaine Conzatti, president of the Christian advocacy group Idaho Family Policy Center, speaks at an anti-abortion rights rally at the Idaho Capitol in 2026 — four years after Idaho’s strict abortion ban went into effect making it illegal in the state.
    
    
        
            <span class="byline">
                Sarah A. Miller
            </span>
        
        
            <span class="credit">smiller@idahostatesman.com</span>
        
    

                        </figcaption>
                    
                
        
    </div></figure><!-- WPS-5038 -- removed the script from WPS and added the placeholder for trinity player --><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!-- CONTENT --><!--[--><!--[--><px-trinity-audio class="" data-defuddle-shadow="

        &lt;svg xmlns=&quot;http://www.w3.org/2000/svg&quot; viewBox=&quot;0 0 640 640&quot;&gt;&lt;path d=&quot;M128 288C128 182 214 96 320 96C426 96 512 182 512 288L512 344.4C495 329.2 472.6 320 448 320L432 320C405.5 320 384 341.5 384 368L384 496C384 522.5 405.5 544 432 544L448 544C501 544 544 501 544 448L544 288C544 164.3 443.7 64 320 64C196.3 64 96 164.3 96 288L96 448C96 501 139 544 192 544L208 544C234.5 544 256 522.5 256 496L256 368C256 341.5 234.5 320 208 320L192 320C167.4 320 145 329.2 128 344.4L128 288zM128 416C128 380.7 156.7 352 192 352L208 352C216.8 352 224 359.2 224 368L224 496C224 504.8 216.8 512 208 512L192 512C156.7 512 128 483.3 128 448L128 416zM512 416L512 448C512 483.3 483.3 512 448 512L432 512C423.2 512 416 504.8 416 496L416 368C416 359.2 423.2 352 432 352L448 352C483.3 352 512 380.7 512 416z&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;

    &lt;/slot&gt;
    "></px-trinity-audio><p>Dr. Kylie Cooper, a maternal-fetal medicine physician, practices in Minnesota after she left Idaho in the spring of 2023, shortly after Idaho’s criminal abortion ban went into effect.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Although it has been nearly three years since she left, Cooper retained her Idaho medical license, she said.</p><connatix-video-ad class=""><yz-videoplayer data-cid="1ffe5d7e-eb53-11e9-b4d2-06948452ae1a" data-pid="799ea453-c864-4a06-b500-a0db0f1550b4" data-player="cnx" data-discovery="true" data-defuddle-shadow="&lt;div class=&quot;yz-player-container&quot;&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;slot&gt;&lt;/slot&gt;"><div class="connatix"><div>TOP VIDEOS</div><cnx class="cnx-main-container cnx-in-desktop cnx-el cnx-mod-no-height" id="beda07bc5fb64a06b53225810166661f" data-compass-skipped="true"><cnx class="cnx-content-frame cnx-size-responsive"><cnx class="cnx-ratio"></cnx><cnx class="cnx-content-wrapper"><cnx class="cnx-video-container"></cnx></cnx></cnx></cnx></div></yz-videoplayer></connatix-video-ad><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“I have a sliver of hope that maybe things will get better in the future,” Cooper said.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>Cooper’s license, as well as every other active medical OB-GYN license in the state, would have shown up in data recently shared by the Idaho Family Policy Center — a Christian advocacy group that lobbied for Idaho’s criminal abortion ban and helped author a 2022 law allowing family members of a fetus aborted after six weeks to <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://idahocapitalsun.com/2022/03/14/texas-style-abortion-bill-heads-to-idaho-gov-brad-littles-desk/">sue the abortion provider</a> for a minimum of $20,000.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>As campaigns for and against <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://archive.sos.idaho.gov/elections/initiatives/2026/RFPA.pdf">Proposition 1</a> — a citizen-led ballot initiative that would repeal the state’s strict abortion ban and provide protections for contraception and in vitro fertilization — ramp up for the Nov. 3 general election, the abortion law’s effect on the number of practicing OB-GYNs in Idaho has become a talking point. But determining the true number of doctors providing obstetrician-gynecologist care in the state isn’t easy.</p><div id="zone-el-102" class="cap-width zone-el" data-type="contentcard"></div><!--]--><!--[--><p>The state has said the number of active licenses do not reliably reflect how many OB-GYNs are currently practicing in Idaho.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The Idaho Family Policy Center has underscored the licensing data to question, and file a <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://idahofamily.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/2026-07-24-Petition-and-Complaint.pdf">lawsuit</a> over, a 2025 Boise State University <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamanetworkopen/fullarticle/2837058">study</a> that found Idaho had lost 35% of its OB-GYNs since 2022, when the U.S. Supreme Court overturned Roe v. Wade and Idaho’s criminal abortion law went into effect.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The policy center on Aug. 3 <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://www.facebook.com/share/p/1BbKZPA2iF/">posted</a> a graph on Facebook showing the number of active licenses increasing from 2022 to 2025, writing, “Pro-abortion advocates are lying to you.”</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“The number of actively licensed OB/GYNs in Idaho has actually increased since our pro-life laws went into effect, according to data from the Idaho Board of Medicine,” the post said.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The center’s posts about the licensure numbers have been shared by Idaho state legislators, such as Twin Falls Republican Rep. Clint Hostetler and Middleton Republican Rep. Tammy Nichols.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><h2 class="h2">Idaho medical board says it has no ‘reliable way’ to count active physicians</h2><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The Idaho Board of Medicine, which is under the state Division of Occupational and Professional Licenses, shows the number of active OB-GYN licenses in Idaho going from around 280 in 2022 to just under 340 in 2024, according to the policy center’s <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://idahofamily.org/pro-abortion-activists-are-lying-obgyns-arent-leaving-idaho-after-all/">graph</a>.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>Bob McLaughlin, spokesperson at the Division of Occupational and Professional Licenses, known as DOPL, told the Idaho Capital Sun that there are limitations to the state’s data to try and determine how many doctors are practicing in the state.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“One, it’s a two-year license,” McLaughlin said in an email. “The number of licenses could fluctuate since the licensee is required to renew on their birthdate every two years and not annually. Two, physician specialty information is self-reported and is not required as part of licensure. DOPL does not have a reliable way to determine how many licensed physicians are actively practicing in Idaho. Three, our records reflect licensure status, and an active license does not necessarily mean an individual is currently practicing.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Family policy center President Blaine Conzatti <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://idahofamily.org/pro-abortion-activists-are-lying-obgyns-arent-leaving-idaho-after-all/">wrote on the organization’s website</a>, noting that the data from the Board of Medicine is “admittedly imperfect,” citing many of the same limitations McLaughlin named, including that not all physicians practice in states where their license is active and that specialties are self-reported.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“Even still, it’s the best data available,” Conzatti wrote. “And it clearly shows that the number of Idaho OB/GYNs hasn’t been decreasing at the rate that JAMA article researchers — and their boosters in the media — have been telling you.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The 2025 study, published in the Journal of the American Medical Association, found that Idaho experienced a net loss of 94 out of 264 OB-GYN physicians between August 2022 and December 2024.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Those numbers were compiled from “from publicly available credentialing files and sources, licensing boards, physician websites, Idaho’s American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists registry, Idaho Medical Association database, local knowledge of the practices, and direct physician confirmation,” the study’s abstract said.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>The researchers tracked relocations, those who stopped practicing obstetrics, died or retired, and counted newly credentialed doctors, the study said. It did not include temporary physicians or family medicine doctors who also practice obstetrics.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“The unique thing about that JAMA Open study was that it was an actual count of actual individuals in Idaho,” said Susie Keller, CEO of the Idaho Medical Association. “It wasn’t an extrapolation. It wasn’t based on estimates. So it was very unique and went through many, many layers of scrutiny before being published.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Keller said physicians left in that time period for a variety of reasons, some of which included Idaho’s abortion laws but others were personal, such as to be closer to family.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“There are a lot of factors that play into our physician shortage,” Keller said. “And for this particular subset, I think the study did a good job of explaining those different reasons.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>A spokesperson from St. Luke’s, which has hospital clinic locations across southwest Idaho and Twin Falls, told the Idaho Capital Sun there were limitations to using licensing data to try and quantify how many doctors there are practicing.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“While we cannot speak for statewide physician licensing trends, our experience is that licensing figures alone do not necessarily reflect the number of physicians actively providing obstetrical care to Idaho patients or available for long-term practice in Idaho,” spokesperson Joy Prudek said in an email.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>She said that licenses may include those who “practice intermittently,” or temporary physicians who fill in gaps, or those who retain licenses from multiple states.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Conzatti told the Sun in an email, that while he knows there are limitations to the licensing data, he questions the JAMA study data.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“We’ve acknowledged from the beginning that the data on actively licensed physicians doesn’t tell the whole story,” he said. “… That being said, we’re willing to bet that the state board data is reliable for our purposes here than the JAMA report for a handful of reasons.”</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>He said one of reasons is the data from the study provided to the policy center from the records request shows two counties with negative total OB-GYNs.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“That’s impossible — and errors in a data set skew the results,” Conzatti said of the negative doctor counts. “These errors also lead us to question the reliability of the other data relied upon in the report. Unfortunately, because BSU has not complied with our public record request to release the underlying data, communications, and review documents, we can’t thoroughly vet the JAMA report to determine if other data errors exist.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>He also questioned the methodology used by the researchers to collect the data.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“From what we can gather, the authors of the report determined whether a doctor had left based in part on internet searches and asking friends, neither of which are known as robustly scientific processes,” Conzatti said. “Unfortunately, because the authors have still not released their full report and have refused to release the documents we requested, we don’t have further insight into the usefulness of the method and the findings of the study.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><h2 class="h2">What are Idaho doctors saying?</h2><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Cooper, the maternal-fetal medicine specialist, said she left Idaho because of the threat of prison time under Idaho’s criminal abortion ban and her fear she wouldn’t be able to provide the care she normally would.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>As a doctor focused on high-risk pregnancies, she said there were too many potential complications that could warrant an abortion that wouldn’t fall into the law’s exception to prevent the death of the mother or in cases of rape and incest within the first trimester, if there’s a police report filed.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“It was a very difficult decision that we made,” Cooper said. “Thinking through a lot of scenarios, thinking through if I went to prison, what would happen to my husband and children? I mean, it’s just very insane conversations that I never anticipated ever having to have.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Idaho has had a shortage of doctors for years, said Keller of the Idaho Medical Association. The association has been part of an effort to try and increase physician training opportunities for more than 10 years.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Keller said OB-GYNs have been the focus of tracking because the abortion ban very directly impacted that specialty.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“This particular law that all of a sudden, made evidence-based practice that many of those OB-GYNs had been providing for their patients for many, many years … one day in 2022, it became illegal,” Keller said. “And it very much changed their ability to practice in Idaho.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Dr. Brenna McCrummen is practicing as an OB-GYN in Coeur d‘Alene, and told the Sun on Monday she feels the pressure from the loss of other specialists in North Idaho. In 2023, Sandpoint’s Bonner General Health <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://idahocapitalsun.com/2023/03/17/citing-staffing-issues-and-political-climate-north-idaho-hospital-will-no-longer-deliver-babies/">closed its labor and delivery unit</a>, citing the political climate and staffing issues.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Many women in the area now must travel for hours to seek care in Coeur d’Alene, McCrummen said. She said this can be especially problematic if a pregnant mom experiences unexpected complications.</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“Those problems can get much, much worse the farther out you are,” she said. “Those outcomes for baby and for mom are much worse, and I have definitely seen people arrive to our hospital much sicker than they should be, and that’s all due to the time that they have to travel.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Prudek, of St. Luke’s, said the health system has made progress in attracting obstetricians and gynecologists, but challenges remain.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>“From St. Luke’s perspective, recruiting and retaining OB-GYN physicians remains a priority,” Prudek said. “We have had some success recruiting OB-GYN physicians, particularly by attracting physicians with ties to Idaho and graduates of regional medical education programs. At the same time, our need for OB-GYN physicians continues to exceed the available workforce, and we have experienced limited success recruiting OB hospitalists.”</p><!--]--><!--[--><h2 class="h2">Why is the family policy center suing BSU over the study?</h2><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The policy center <a target="_blank" rel="Follow" href="https://www.idahoednews.org/news/lobbying-group-sues-boise-state-alleging-it-withheld-public-records/">filed its lawsuit</a> against Boise State University in Ada County District Court on July 24, Idaho EdNews reported.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The advocacy group had sought records related to the 2025 study and its data. Its request returned only a two-page document with the raw data, showing a decline in OB-GYNs.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Conzatti said in the family policy center post announcing the legal challenge that the biggest question for them remained unanswered, “Are OB/GYNs actually leaving the state, as the JAMA study claims? Or is the number of OB/GYNs practicing in Idaho actually increasing, as the data from the Idaho Board of Medicine seems to indicate?”</p><!--]--><!--[--><p>“This lawsuit means that we’ll finally get the answers,” Conzatti wrote. “And with the upcoming abortion ballot initiative, the need for accurate information has never been more crucial.”</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>The complaint alleges the university is withholding records, that the response came well after the 10-day deadline required by Idaho public records laws, and that it should not have charged the policy center $257.02 for legal review of the two-page document it received.</p><!----><!--]--><!--[--><p>Stephany Galbreaith, director of media relations at Boise State, told Idaho EdNews, “We do not comment on pending litigation.”</p><rate-article-experience class="" data-defuddle-shadow="

    .acrobat-button-container {
        position: fixed;
        right: 0;
        bottom: 36px;
        z-index: 2147483646;
        visibility: visible !important;
        pointer-events: all;
        opacity: 1;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        transition: opacity 0.3s ease-in-out;
    }

    @media print {
        .acrobat-button-container {
            display: none !important;
        }
    }

    .acrobat-button {
        position: relative;
        background: white;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        pointer-events: all;
        touch-action: none;
        box-shadow: 0px 2px 8px 0px #00000029;
        height: 34px;
        width: 34px;
        border: 1px solid transparent;
        background-clip: padding-box, border-box;
        background-origin: border-box;
        background-image: linear-gradient(white, white),
                        linear-gradient(95.85deg, #D73220 0%, #D92361 33%, #7155FA 100%);
        border-radius: 18px;
        justify-content: right;
        margin-right: var(--fab-margin-right, 28px);
        overflow: hidden;
    }

    .acrobat-button-container-transparent {
        opacity: 0.4 !important;
        transition: opacity 0.3s ease-in-out;
    }

    .acrobat-button-container-transparent:hover,
    .acrobat-button-container-transparent:focus,
    .acrobat-button-container-transparent:active {
        opacity: 1;
    }

    .acrobat-button &gt; svg {
        flex-shrink: 0;
        margin: 4px 5px 5px 5px;
        width: 22px;
        height: 22px;
        pointer-events: none;
        fill: #b40000;
    }
    .acrobat-button:has(.close-btn.open),
    .expand-acrobat-button {
        width: 64px;
    }

    .disabled {
        background: #B1B1B1;
    }

    .tooltip-text {
        width: 160px;
        visibility: hidden;
        opacity: 0;
        background-color: #FFFFFF;
        color: #292929;
        border-radius: 7px;
        padding: 4px 1px 5px 9px;
        position: absolute;
        z-index: 1;
        right: 100%;
        top: 50%;
        transform: translateY(-50%);
        margin-right: 10px;
        transition: opacity 0.3s, visibility 0s linear 2s;
        font-size: 12px;
        line-height: 15px;
        font-family: 'adobe-clean', Adobe Clean, sans-serif;
        filter: drop-shadow(0px 1px 6px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12));
    }

    .tooltip-text::after {
       content: '';
       position: absolute;
       top: 50%;
       left: 100%;
       transform: translateY(-50%);
       border-width: 6px;
       border-style: solid;
       border-color: transparent transparent transparent #fff;
    }

    .show-tooltip {
        visibility: visible;
        opacity: 1;
        line-height: 1.5;
        font-family: 'adobe-clean', Adobe Clean, sans-serif;
        transition: opacity 0.3s;
        transition-delay: 500ms;
    }

    .close-btn {
        position: relative;
        display: none;
        align-items: center;
        justify-content: center;
    }

    .close-btn svg {
        fill: #464646;
    }

    .close-btn.showCloseButton {
        opacity: 1;
        display: flex;
        justify-content: center;
        width: 24px;
        height: 24px;
        padding: 2px;
    }

    .fab-view-settings-dialog {
        display: none;
        position: absolute;
        bottom: 40px;
        z-index: 1;
    }

    .close-btn:hover {
        background: #E6E6E6;
        border-radius: 50%; /* Keep original circular hover */
        cursor: pointer;
    }

    .fab-view-settings-dialog.showDialog {
        display: block;
    }
    .close-btn .burger-icon {
        display: inline;
        margin-bottom: 2px;
    }
    .close-btn .close-icon {
        display: none;
    }
    .close-btn.open .burger-icon {
        display: none;
    }
    .close-btn.open .close-icon {
        display: inline;
    }
    .tooltip-text .tooltip-text-content {
        font-weight: bold;
    }
    .tooltip-text .tooltip-sub-text-text {
        font-style: italic;
        font-size: 11px;
    }

    /* Home tooltip FTE — icon + layout overrides */
    .fab-home-tooltip {
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        gap: 12px;
        width: 183px;
        border-radius: 8px;
        padding: 6px 8px 8px 12px;
        font-family: 'adobe-clean', Adobe Clean, sans-serif;
        box-shadow: 0px 2px 4px rgba(0,0,0,0.08), 0px 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.04), 0px 0px 1px rgba(0,0,0,0.08), 0px 3px 5px rgba(116,23,208,0.07);
    }
    .fab-home-tooltip .tooltip-text-content {
        font-family: 'adobe-clean', Adobe Clean, sans-serif;
        font-weight: 500;
        font-size: 12px;
        line-height: 16px;
        color: #292929;
        white-space: normal;
        word-wrap: break-word;
        width: 145px;
    }
    .fab-home-tooltip-icon {
        flex-shrink: 0;
        display: block;
        width: 16px;
        height: 16px;
    }

    /* Icon container styles - replaces iframe */
    .icon-container {
        width: 33px;
        height: 34px;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        justify-content: center;
        cursor: grab;
        pointer-events: all;
        flex-shrink: 0;
        background: transparent;
        border: none;
    }

    .icon-container:active {
        cursor: grabbing;
    }

    .fab-svg-icon {
        width: 22px;
        height: 22px;
        padding: 5px 5px 5px 6px;
        pointer-events: none;
        fill: #b40000;
    }

    .draggable-handle {
        display: none;
        position: absolute;
        right: var(--drag-handle-right, 12px);
        cursor: grab;
    }
    .draggable-handle-visible {
        display: flex;
    }

    /* Font face declarations - for proper font loading in Shadow DOM */
    @font-face {
        font-family: 'adobe-clean';
        font-weight: 400;
        src: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/css/fonts/AdobeClean-Regular.otf') format('opentype');
    }
    @font-face {
        font-family: 'adobe-clean';
        font-weight: 700;
        src: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/css/fonts/AdobeClean-Bold.otf') format('opentype');
    }
    @font-face {
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Regular';
        src: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/css/fonts/AdobeClean-Regular.otf') format('opentype');
    }
    @font-face {
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Bold';
        src: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/css/fonts/AdobeClean-Bold.otf') format('opentype');
    }
    body {
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Regular', adobe-clean, &quot;Adobe Clean&quot;, sans-serif;
    }

    .dialog-container {
        width: 166px;
        background-color: #ffffff;
        border-radius: 4px;
        border: 1px solid #B1B1B1;
        box-shadow: 0px 1px 4px 0px #00000026;
        z-index: 1000;
        padding: 4px;
        position: relative;
        margin: 4px 0px;
    }

    .menu {
        display: flex;
        flex-direction: column;
        gap: 4px;
        list-style-type: none;
        margin: 0;
        padding: 0;
    }

    .menu .menu_item {
        cursor: pointer;
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Regular', adobe-clean, &quot;Adobe Clean&quot;, sans-serif;
        align-content: center;
        font-size: 14px;
        font-weight: 400;
        line-height: 18.2px;
        text-align: left;
        color: #222222;
        padding: 0px 12px;
        height: 32px;
        width: 140px;
    }

    .menu .menu_item:hover {
        background-color: #eaeaea;
    }

    .preferences {
        font-size: 12px;
        font-weight: 400;
        line-height: 18.2px;
        text-align: left;
        color: #464646;
    }

    .divider {
        border-top: 1px solid #E6E6E6;
    }

    /* React-specific wrappers - minimal */
    .fab-root {
        font-family: 'adobe-clean', Adobe Clean, sans-serif;
    }

    button.close-btn {
        background: transparent;
        border: none;
        cursor: pointer;
        padding: 0;
        z-index: 10; /* Ensure clickable above all other elements */
        pointer-events: auto;
    }

    /* FAB Action Pills - From Figma Design */
    .fab-pills-container {
        position: absolute;
        bottom: 42px; /* FAB height (34px) + gap (8px) = 42px */
        right: var(--fab-margin-right, 28px);
        display: flex;
        flex-direction: column;
        gap: 8px;
        align-items: flex-end;
        width: 142px;
        z-index: 1;
    }

    .fab-pills-container.pills-below {
        bottom: auto;
        top: 42px; /* Same gap when positioned below */
    }

    /* Extend hover area using ::after pseudo-element - doesn't affect visual layout */
    .fab-pills-container::after {
        content: '';
        position: absolute;
        top: 100%; /* Start right after the pills content */
        left: 0;
        right: 0;
        height: 8px;
        background: transparent;
        pointer-events: auto;
    }

    .fab-pills-container.pills-below::after {
        top: auto;
        bottom: 100%; /* Start right before the pills when positioned below */
    }

    /* FTE Header - First Time Experience (56px with close button) */
    .fab-fte-header {
        position: relative;
        width: 142px;
        min-height: 56px;
        background: #FFFFFF;
        border-radius: 12px;
        box-shadow: 0px 4px 12px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.08), 0px 2px 6px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.04), 0px 0px 2px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12);
        overflow: hidden;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
    }

    .fab-fte-background {
        position: absolute;
        left: -13px;
        top: 50%;
        transform: translateY(-50%);
        width: 183.33px;
        height: 126.82px;
        background: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/images/fab-header-background.svg') no-repeat center;
        background-size: cover;
        pointer-events: none;
        z-index: 0;
    }

    .fab-fte-close {
        position: absolute;
        top: 0;
        right: 0;
        width: 24px;
        height: 24px;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        justify-content: center;
        background: transparent;
        border: none;
        cursor: pointer;
        z-index: 2;
        color: #222222;
        padding: 0;
    }

    .fab-fte-close:hover {
        background: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.05);
        border-radius: 12px;
    }

    .fab-fte-close svg {
        width: 8px;
        height: 8px;
    }

    .fab-fte-text {
        position: relative;
        width: 102px;
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Bold', adobe-clean, sans-serif;
        font-weight: 400; /* Use Bold font file directly, not synthetic bold */
        font-size: 12px;
        line-height: 16px;
        color: #222222; /* Figma spec color */
        z-index: 1;
        white-space: normal;
        word-wrap: break-word;
        margin-left: 16px;
        margin-right: auto;
        box-sizing: border-box;
        -webkit-font-smoothing: subpixel-antialiased; /* Better font rendering */
        -moz-osx-font-smoothing: auto;
    }

    /* Hover Header - Normal state (24px without close button) */
    .fab-hover-header {
        position: relative;
        width: 142px;
        min-height: 24px;
        background: #FFFFFF; /* White background to show the colorful SVG and text */
        border-radius: 12px;
        box-shadow: 0px 4px 12px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.08), 0px 2px 6px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.04), 0px 0px 2px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12);
        overflow: hidden;
        cursor: pointer;
    }

    .fab-hover-background {
        position: absolute;
        left: -27px;
        top: 50%;
        transform: translateY(-50%);
        width: 212.63px;
        height: 147.09px;
        background: url('chrome-extension://efaidnbmnnnibpcajpcglclefindmkaj/browser/images/fab-header-background.svg') no-repeat center;
        background-size: cover;
        pointer-events: none;
        z-index: 0;
    }

    .fab-hover-text {
        position: relative;
        width: 100%;
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        justify-content: center;
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Bold', adobe-clean, sans-serif;
        font-weight: 400; /* Use Bold font file directly, not synthetic bold */
        font-size: 12px;
        line-height: 15px;
        color: #222222; /* Figma spec color */
        z-index: 1;
        white-space: normal;
        padding: 4px 9px;
        box-sizing: border-box;
        -webkit-font-smoothing: subpixel-antialiased; /* Better font rendering */
        -moz-osx-font-smoothing: auto;
    }

    /* Individual Pill Buttons */
    .fab-pill-button {
        display: flex;
        flex-direction: row;
        align-items: center;
        gap: 0;
        width: 142px;
        min-height: 24px; /* Min height, allows growth with content */
        background: #FFFFFF;
        border: none;
        border-radius: 12px;
        box-shadow: 0px 4px 12px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.08), 0px 2px 6px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.04), 0px 0px 2px 0px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12);
        cursor: pointer;
        padding: 0;
        transition: background 0.2s ease;
        overflow: visible;
    }

    .fab-pill-button:hover {
        background: #D5D5D5;
    }

    .fab-pill-button:hover .fab-pill-label,
    .fab-pill-button:hover .fab-pill-icon {
        color: #000000; /* Hover state color from Figma Alias/content/neutral/hover */
    }

    .fab-pill-button:active {
        background: #F4F4F4;
    }

    .fab-pill-icon {
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        justify-content: center;
        padding: 4px 7px 4px 10px; /* Exact Figma specs: top right bottom left */
        flex-shrink: 0;
        box-sizing: border-box;
    }

    .fab-pill-icon img {
        width: 16px;
        height: 16px;
        display: block;
        flex-shrink: 0;
        /* SVG icons with currentColor fill will inherit color from parent */
        filter: brightness(0) saturate(100%) invert(13%) sepia(0%) saturate(0%) hue-rotate(196deg) brightness(97%) contrast(97%); /* #222222 */
    }

    .fab-pill-button:hover .fab-pill-icon img {
        filter: brightness(0) saturate(100%); /* #000000 on hover */
    }

    .fab-pill-label {
        display: flex;
        align-items: center;
        font-family: 'AdobeClean-Bold', adobe-clean, sans-serif;
        font-weight: 400; /* Use Bold font file directly, not synthetic bold */
        font-size: 12px;
        line-height: 15px; /* 1.252 * 12px = 15.024px */
        color: #222222; /* Default text color from Figma Alias/content/neutral/default */
        padding: 4px 9px 5px 0; /* Reduced right padding to prevent wrapping */
        text-align: left;
        white-space: normal; /* Allow text wrapping for multi-line content */
        box-sizing: border-box;
        flex: 1;
        overflow: visible;
        -webkit-font-smoothing: subpixel-antialiased; /* Better font rendering */
        -moz-osx-font-smoothing: auto;
    }
